# Names and comments for tools/disasm.py, keyed by ROM address.
# ADDR<TAB>NAME<TAB>COMMENT. NAME (optional) replaces the label at ADDR;
# COMMENT is printed above the line at ADDR. Several lines may share an
# address. These are our own notes, not data taken from the ROM.
# Named from the code, by reading each and its callers (most-used unnamed objects first).
# Second round.
# Third round.
00000		Reset entry: P=3, then on to RESET (#01FEA) and the boot code at #7018C
0000F	Interrupt	Interrupt handler entry
002DA	WaitMPClear	-> : a card was pulled (MP set): keep writing #C to the card control register #0010E, after a short delay, until MP stays clear
002FF	BatSleep	ST0 = 1 when the very-low-battery bit caused this: go through the stub at #70250 (blanks the display, serial off), then SHUTDN in a loop until the battery is fine and the machine is woken
003C0	SrvcSerial	-> : interrupt service for the serial port: if it is on (IOC bit 3) and a byte waits, move received bytes into the ring buffer at #80319 (status at #80519), handling XON/XOFF, and wait out one character time before giving up
00487		Time-out in TIMER2 ticks for one character at each baud code
005EC	SrvcTimer1	-> : TIMER1 interrupt: if TIMER1 went negative, count down the nibble at #808CC and re-arm the timer while a key is held or the bit at #80805 asks for it; otherwise stop TIMER1
00655	ArmKeyPoll	-> : TIMER1 = 0 with wake and interrupt enabled (control 6): it fires at the next 1/16 s tick, which is how a held key gets scanned again
00672	CkLowBat	-> : read the battery and card status, update the low-battery nibble #80840, set or clear the alert bit in the annunciator byte #80841, and refresh the annunciators
006ED	ReadLowBat	C.A = mask of bits wanted -> A.B = bits of #00108, sampled six times with the detector enabled (#00109 = #C, then 4 again); carry set if bit 1 (main battery low) is on
00735	WaitCardStat	-> C.XS = card status (#0010F), read after writing #C to #0010E and waiting for 5 ticks of TIMER2 so the lines settle
00768		Keyboard interrupt: nothing to do while #8065B says scanning is off; otherwise read the matrix (BITMAP), debounce, and hand changes to SrvcKbdAB
009E1	KeyShiftBits	A.W = key map from BITMAP -> C.S = three bits: the alpha, left and right shift keys held (map bits 23, 17, 11)
00A09	ShiftBits>Plane	C.S = held shift bits -> C.A = their keycode plane in the high nibble of a byte; carry set if the combination is not allowed
00A2F		Plane nibble by value of C.S; F marks a combination that is refused
00A37		Probably: after a key scan, decides if the 1/16 s TIMER1 tick is close enough that a rescan is not needed (carry set); carry clear makes SrvcKbdAB scan again
00A89	KeyBitCodes	
00C8D	KeyDown?	A.B = keycode 1-49, or #80 for alpha, #40 for left shift -> carry set if that key is held now. ON (#2D) and bad codes return carry clear. The matrix table gives the OUT and IN masks
00D14	LShiftDown?	-> carry set if left shift is held (OUT bit 2, IN bit 5)
00D1D	RShiftDown?	-> carry set if right shift is held (OUT bit 1, IN bit 5)
00D26	AlphaDown?	-> carry set if alpha is held (OUT bit 3, IN bit 5)
00D3B	ClrKeyState	-> : clear the 13-nibble map at #8065C of keys already seen as down
00D9F	KeyBufPut	C.B = keycode -> carry set if the key buffer is full; otherwise append it. #80669 is the read index, #8066A the write index, entries of 2 nibbles follow
00DF8	KbdOut0	-> carry set if IN # 0: drive no OUT line (only ON can read), remembering it in #80642
00E0B	KbdOutAll	-> carry set if IN # 0: drive all nine OUT lines, remembering it in #80642
00E21	KbdOutC	C.X = OUT lines -> carry set if IN # 0: store C.X in #80642 (the interrupt code restores OUT from there), set OUT and read IN
00E38	OnKeyCombo	ON is down: match the keyboard map against OnKeyTable until all keys are released. The return address pushed here counts one ATTN in #807F7; a combination replaces it
00ED5	OnKeyTable	Entries: kind (1 run now, 2 and 3 run on release), key map as BITMAP gives it, handler. Keys by map bit: 0 ON, 1 +, 2 SPC, 6 -, 9 1, 10 A, 15 4, 44 F, 45 E, 46 D, 47 C, 48 B
00ED6		ON + : contrast up. Next entry, ON - : contrast down
00EFC		ON B : no action, no ATTN counted
00F0F		ON A F : cold start
00F22		ON 1 : print the LCD
00F35		ON 4 : set DOUSEALARM (#80834), which stops a sounding alarm
00F48		ON C : warm start. Then ON D : interactive self-test, ON E : continuous self-test, ON SPC : deep sleep
00F9C		Contrast is 5 bits at #00101 and stays within #09..#18. P=0 here means down: P=P-1 then sets carry
00FF8		ON released on its own: count one ATTN, without letting the counter wrap
01011	OnPrintLCD	Save the return stack, R1 and R2 in IRAM at #8069C, print the LCD, and restore them
01087	OnDeepSleep	Beep, sleep (BatSleep with ST0 clear), clear #80010.. and restart with reset code 0
010A9	ClrWSLog	-> : clear the warm-start log: four 18-nibble entries at #80010 (see LogWarmstart)
010CE		ON 4 handler
01145	ContrastWait	-> : idle loop of #597F turns, so a held ON + or ON - steps the contrast slowly
0119D	RebootIO	-> : reset the bus, reconfigure memory, clear the bit at #80805 and reinitialise the hardware. First step of the self-tests
011AC	ResetConfig	-> : RESET, then configure I/O, RAM and the ports again (ST15 clear: interrupts held off)
011CA	ClrPollBit	-> : clear bit 1 of the nibble at #80805, the one that keeps TIMER1 polling in SrvcTimer1
011FC	GetTimeRaw	-> C.W = time in ticks of 1/8192 s since year 0 (the offset at #80058 minus TIMER2); carry set if the clock is not to be trusted: TIMER2 stopped, the CRC at #80065 is wrong, or the time is outside 1991-2090. A.W, B.W, D.W clobbered
012F9	GetTimeNoInt	-> C.W: GetTimeRaw with interrupts held off (ST15), then AllowIntr
01485	SetT2WakeClk	ST0 from ClkOn>ST0, then SetT2Wake
0148C	SetT2Wake	Reprogram TIMER2 for the next event; a bad clock restarts through the memory-lost path
01496	SetT2WakeC	C.W = time now in ticks. Choose the next wake-up: at most 3 days ahead (1 hour if #80077 is set), the time at #80069 if earlier, a due alarm in the ALARMS list, or the next whole second while the clock shows (ST0); then wrap the clock into 1991-2090 and load TIMER2 (done at TryToRM?.L9)
014B7		Longest wait: #7E900000 ticks is 3 days
014DD		#1C20000 ticks is 1 hour
016E9	SetClockOffs	A.W = clock offset (the time at TIMER2 = 0) -> stored at #80058 with its CRC at #80065; D0 kept
01702	SetClockOffsD1	As SetClockOffs with D1 already at #80058: the CRC circuit computes the check while the 13 nibbles are read back
0172B	SetT2WakeOb	-> : code object: run SetT2Wake with interrupts held off, so that a new or changed alarm takes effect
01750	ClockMaxTicks	-> C.W = #1EBA1AC100000: ticks at the end of the clock's range (1 January 2091)
01763	ClockMinTicks	-> C.W = #1D41E89A00000: ticks at the start of the clock's range (1 January 1991)
01776	Ticks100Years	-> C.W = #178322700000: ticks in 100 years (36525 days). The clock wraps by this much at the end of its range
01789	Ticks1Min	-> C.W = #78000: ticks in one minute
01797	Ticks10Min	-> C.W = #4B0000: ticks in 10 minutes
01929	InitIO	-> : power-up: sane contrast (#09..#18, default #0E), annunciators on, restart the timers and clock, enable keyboard and interrupts, clear and show the display, then fall into T1Nap
019DA	T1Nap	-> : SHUTDN until TIMER1 has run down from 1 or the very-low-battery bit is set, then TIMER1 = 7; the timer control byte is kept
01A33	InitClock	-> : clear the next-alarm time, restart TIMER2 from the saved clock. A bad clock reports warm-start reason 4 and starts at 1 January 1991. TIMER2 is loaded so the first event comes within a minute
01A96	SetNextAlarm	A.W = time -> stored at #80069; #80076 is set to F
01ABD	SetT2Awake	C.P = nibble -> stored at #80077: zero before turning off, F after waking; nonzero makes SetT2WakeC wake at least every hour
01B66	KbdOutRestore	-> A.A = IN: set OUT back from #80642 and allow interrupts; D1 kept. Part of the beeper
01BEB	BusyAnnOn	-> : light the BUSY annunciator in the hardware (ANNCTRL bit 4)
01C23	FillD0W	A.W = value, C.A = count-1, D0 -> : write count 16-nibble words, D0 past them
01D44	DispRestore	-> : wait, turn the display off, and restore DISPADDR, line offset and line count from the copies at #8068D, #80692 and #8069A
01DA1	RamSizeTest	B.A = RAM base -> carry clear with C = "128K" or " 32K" text, D0 past the word tested; carry set if offset #3FFFB does not hold data. Offsets that mirror each other mean 32K. Words touched are restored
01E5E	ShowHeldAnn	-> : code object: read the alpha and shift keys and light the annunciator of the one held (none if two or more) on top of the byte at #80841. Machine code enters at the GOSUB target below
01E79		Bits 6, 5, 4 of the annunciator byte are alpha, right and left shift; the XS nibble counts keys held
01EFD	AnnByte>HW	A.B = annunciator byte (#80841 layout) -> : map it through the table below and write it to ANNCTRL, keeping the two top bits; B.A kept
01F53	ArmKeyPollOb	-> : code object: ArmKeyPoll, D0 kept
021C5	RunFirstProc	-> : code object run after boot: keyboard state cleared, input stream and INITEN set, then the object in FIRSTPROC runs
02241		Boot: configure the I/O page
02241	ConfigIO	-> : CONFIG the I/O page at #00100
0224F		Boot: probe RAM by writing #80000 and looking for a mirror at #90000, then configure 128K or 32K
0224F	ConfigRAM	-> : see the note above: configure the RAM at #80000 at its size
0229F	ConfigRAMAC	C.A = RAM address, A.A = size mask -> : CONFIG both, and keep the mask and address in the IRAM shadows
022D9	ConfigBootRAM	-> : CONFIG RAM at #C0000 with a 64K mask, and note that in the IRAM shadows there. Used while the self-test code in ROM at #80000 must stay visible
02313	RecoverPrompt	-> : memory was lost at boot: default contrast, mark the top of RAM, and if ST7 is set show "Try To Recover Memory?" and wait for YES (key A) or NO (key F); other keys beep. Without ST7 memory is cleared at once
02388	ErrBeep	-> : short error beep
02395	ClrMemBoot	NO: clear the screen, then build an empty home directory and all the system pointers
023ED		YES: show the recovering message and try to rebuild from what is in RAM (ST7 set)
0245E	DefaultFlags	-> : default system flags
0249F	InitDispGrobs	-> : empty ROMPTAB, then build the display grobs after it: the menu grob VDISP2 (8 x 131), the stack grob ADISP (56 x 131) and an empty GDISP, and set VDISP, VDISP3 and TEMPOB
02519	MakeGrob	C.A = address, D1 = pointer variable, A = 15 nibbles (length, height, width) -> C.A past the new grob: write the pointer and the grob header
02537	DispPtrs-	C.A = distance -> : DispPtrs+ with the distance negated
02541	DispPtrs+	C.A = distance -> : add it to every pointer to the display grobs and to the hardware DISPADDR and MENUADDR, after memory has moved
02593	InitMemPtrs	-> : lay out empty tables after USEROB: CONTEXT, STOPSIGN, TOUCHTAB, DOLPENV, TEMPENV, EDITLINE, the data stack DSKTOP, TEMPTOP and RSKTOP, each ended by its zero words
0266F	ClrIntrpPtrs	-> : zero those of the 37 saved pointers at INTRPPTR that point at TEMPOB up to USEROB or at ROMPARTS and above, since those objects did not survive
026CA	CallTestROM	C.A = address -> : call code in the self-test ROM at #80000: move RAM to #C0000 so ROM shows through, run it, and move RAM back
02708	LogWarmstart	P = reason -> : push a new entry on the warm-start log that WSLOG shows: entries of reason (1), time (13) and CRC (4) at #80010, the oldest dropped. A bad clock logs time 0. P kept
027A2	BootKeyWait	-> carry set if ON was pressed, else clear with a key in the buffer: wait for a key during boot, in lite sleep, with alarms off. TIMER2 is set for 10 minutes; if it runs out the calculator goes to deep sleep and waits again
02F6F		ob -> ob: after an id is evaluated: a directory (DORRP) is entered through CONTEXT!, otherwise ATTN is checked and the object runs
03012	SKIPOBA	A.A = first word of the object, D0 past it -> D0 past the object. SKIPOB with the word already read
03416	GC1	-> : SafeGargb asking for one word
037F5	ArrayType	C.A = array -> A.A = its element prolog; ST3 clear for an array (DOARRY), set for a linked array. D1 kept
0381C	DimCount	A.A = :: of dimensions -> D.A = their count, D0 at the SEMI. A dimension is an inline bint (DOBINT and value) or a pointer
0384F	DimProduct	A.A = :: of dimensions, D.A = count -> A.A = product; carry set on overflow
03878	DimNext	D0 at a dimension's slot before -> C.A = value of the next dimension, inline bint or pointer to one
038A0	ArrayElAddr	C.A = array, A.A = element number from 1, ST3 and R2 as from ArrayType -> D0 at the element. Linked arrays have a table of 5-nibble offsets; fixed-size types multiply by their size (bint 5+5, real 16, ...); others are skipped one by one
03A2E	RoomOrGC	C.A = nibbles needed -> : make sure ROOM has them, collecting garbage once; otherwise Insufficient Memory
03A64	GetINITEN	-> ST2 = 1 if the low nibble of INITEN was set; that nibble is cleared
042B8	PushT1Ctl	-> : push the timer control bytes on the return stack, under the return address
042CC	PopT1Ctl	-> : restore the timer control bytes pushed by PushT1Ctl
042E0	PreOff	-> : before turning off: clear the next-alarm time and the awake mode, reprogram TIMER2, stop TIMER1 and the serial port, and restore the display registers
0431D	T2Int>ST11	C.XS = TIMER2 control -> ST11 = its interrupt bit
0432B	OffChecks	-> : before turning off: restart if the cards changed, check the config and port CRCs
04337	OffWait	-> ST12 set if ON woke the machine: annunciators off, keyboard idle and buffer flushed, then SHUTDN until something wakes it
04391	ShutdnInRAM	-> : write SHUTDN and RTN into RAM at #80524 and run them there: the ROM may be powered down while the CPU sleeps
043C9	WakeChecks	-> : after waking: check the cards and the CRCs; a failure restarts with reason 5
043E0	WakeSort	-> carry clear to carry on running; carry set to go back to sleep: after waking, service TIMER2 and the battery. Unless ON (ST12) or a timer interrupt (ST11) woke it, return; otherwise wait for ON to be released and show the display
0443A	CkCards	-> : compare the card status with PortStat; a change restarts through the interrupt code's path
04457	CkConfCRC	-> : compare COMPCONFCRC with the stored word; a mismatch goes to DEEPSLEEP's restart
0446D	CkPort1CRC	-> carry set if nothing to do or the CRC is the same; carry clear if it changed: if the flags at #8052F say port 1 needs it, compute the CRC of its 128K or 32K and store it in Port1CRC. The test at #044BF only leaves its result in carry
044D4	CRCRange	C.A = start, B.A = end: CRCBlock with the length made from B-C
044D6	CRCBlock	C.A = start, B.A = length in nibbles -> C.A = CRC. Reads the two halves of the block through the CRC circuit 16 nibbles at a time, as the self-test does. A.A = CRC with the top nibble adjusted
045A0	LowBat$Fill	D0 at a 22-character string body, R1.P = bits (0 alarm due, 1 system battery, 2 port 1, 3 port 2): write the line "LowBat(S,P1,P2) Alarm" with spaces after it
04661	PadSpaces	D0, D.A = characters left -> : pad with spaces
04676	PutComma	D0, D.A -> : append a comma
0467A	PutChar	C.B = character, D0, D.A -> : append it and count it
04684	Put2Chars	C = two characters, D0, D.A -> : append them and count them
04698		Restart point: reset the keyboard state and input stream, then run FIRSTPROC
0477E	KbdResume	-> : drive all OUT lines with the keyboard scan held off, clear KEYSTATE if no key is down, then re-enable and raise a scan with RSI
04890	IdleSleep	-> : the idle step of key wait: show held shifts, keep the clock display up, and sleep lightly until something happens
048A9	IdleClock	-> : update the clock in the status area if it is on, and drop it when the status area has gone
049B8	AlarmDue?	-> carry set if an alarm is due and alarm servicing is not off (flag test at #801)
049DB	TimedOut?	-> carry set if the top nibble of TIMEOUTCLK is 0
049ED	UartPending?	-> carry set if bytes wait in the serial buffer and the flag coded #202 is set
04A4C	SETDF	ob #menukey -> : store ob as key #menukey's DF action in TOUCHTAB
04A57	SETPROC	ob #menukey -> : store ob as key #menukey's PROC action in TOUCHTAB, making or growing that table if needed
04B15	TouchTab@	P = table selector (8 DF, 4 PROC, 2, 1) -> D0 at the table in TOUCHTAB, B.A = entries per table, C.S top bit set if this table exists. TOUCHTAB starts with one nibble of table bits and a count byte
04B76	TouchEntry	A.A = table address, R0 = key -> D0 at that key's 5-nibble entry
04B8A	TouchEntry2	As TouchEntry for the 10-nibble entries of table 1
04B94	TouchTabSize	B.A = entries, P -> C.A = nibbles of one table (doubled for table 1)
04BA5	TouchGrowSize	B.A = old count, R0 = new count -> R1 = nibbles added per plain table, D.A = total for all tables present
04BEC	RoomOrGCP	C.A = nibbles needed -> : as RoomOrGC, keeping P and leaving D.A = the amount
04C2C	TouchAddTab	Insert the table selected by P, filled with F, and set its bit in TOUCHTAB
04C70	TouchGrowAll	Grow all four tables to the count in R0 and store the count
04CAE	TouchGrowTab	Grow the table selected by P, if present, by the amount in R1
04E89	ProtectEnv	D0 at TEMPENV or DOLPENV -> : count one more error trap in the protection word of the top environment
04F19	UnprotEnvs	-> : error unwinding: drop temporary and DO-loop environments down to the newest with a nonzero protection word, and count that word down
05204	CopyBody	D0 at a pointer to a string-like object, D1 -> : copy its body to D1 by MOVEDOWN, D1 past it
05CF5	Get2Bodies	-> A = first 16 nibbles of the body of level 1, also in R0, C.A = pointer to level 2, D0 at level 1's body, D1 on level 2. After SAVPTR
05F91	RefInRange?	B.A = low, D.A = high -> carry set if a pointer into [B,D) is on the data stack, return stack or at INTRPPTR; then D1 is at it. ST0 set
06076	GCMark	C.A = pointer, B.A = TEMPOB, A.A = TEMPTOP -> : garbage collector, mark phase: a pointer into TEMPOB finds the end of its object by skipping, and raises the top nibble of the link word there. The return address stays on the stack
060D5	GCAdjPtr	C.A = pointer, D1 at it -> : garbage collector, update phase: a pointer into TEMPOB is moved by the offset the sweep left in the link word of its object
0681E	PtrAdj	C.A = pointer, D1 at it, D.A = distance -> carry set: add the distance if the pointer lies in [A,B); with ST2 also tests the range in R0, R1 and the distance in D0
06863	AdjRefs	C.A = distance, A.A, B.A = range -> C.A: add the distance to every pointer into the range: data stack, return stack, TEMPENV, TOUCHTAB and the system pointers from VDISP on (#36 of them, twice over). Interrupts see no half-done state as PADJSAVE holds the return addresses
0686E	AdjRefs2	As AdjRefs with a second range in R0, R1 and distance in D0 (ST2 set): two blocks that trade places
06B00	TempInsert	B.A = size, C.A = free nibbles, D1 at RSKTOP's value -> D0 at the new slot: open a TEMPOB slot at TEMPTOP by moving the return stack up, write its link word, and update AVMEM and TEMPTOP
06BD6		Probably: move a block towards lower addresses past others, using the free room (A.A) as a buffer: A.A = free, C.A = size, D0 and D1 the two ends; calls AdjRefs2 first. See also the next routine for the other direction
06CD7		Probably: move a block towards higher addresses past others, with the free room as a buffer, as the routine before does the other way
06F30	RstkNextOb	D0 at a return-stack slot + 5 -> C = PRLG, A.A = the code word of the next object in that stream, D0 at the object: the saved pointer steps past the object
07035	Rest>::	-> A.A = new ::, a copy of what remains of the stream on top of the return stack, from the pointer there to its SEMI
070F7	JumpIndC	C.A = address -> : PC=(A) through C
072D7	D0>Index1	A.A saved in D0 -> D0 at the index of the innermost DO loop, A.A = the D0 it had
072EC	D0>Index2	A.A saved in D0 -> D0 at the index of the next DO loop out
07304	StkAddP	C.A = distance, P = levels-1 -> : add the distance to the top pointers on the data stack, then ADJMEM
0758C		R0 = count, D0 at count pointers, D1 -> : copy them to every second 5-nibble slot from D1+10: part of BIND
077B2	Home#Lib	#libnum -> A.A = number (11 bits), B.A = attachment count, D0 and D1 in HOME's header. After SAVPTR
07831	NameDiff?	D0, D1 at counted names -> carry set if they differ; carry clear with D0 past the name if they match
0788E	DirFindName	R1 = directory, R0 = id body -> : walk the directory's names from the last, back to the first; found goes on to GrclR01A, not found returns carry set
078CC	UpDir	R1 = directory -> carry set if it is STOPSIGN, else R1 = its parent (GETRRP)
079D7	ContextOut	ob -> ob: code object: if ob is a directory holding CONTEXT or STOPSIGN, they are sent to HOME. Used before a directory is purged or moved
07A56	LibFindName	D0 at a library, R0 at a counted name -> R3 = command number, carry clear if found; carry set if not. Compares 16 nibbles at a time through the hash table, entering the library through its access handler if it lies in a covered bank
07AB7	JumpC	C.A = address -> : PC=C. A GOSUB here is a computed call
07B6A	LibAccessEnd	-> : P=1: if ST1 says the library needed its access handler, call the handler to cover it again
07B79	MakeROMPTR	R2 = library (with the rest of the word), R3 = command -> A.A = new ROMPTR in TEMPOB
07BAF	MakeIDAt	D0 at a counted name -> A.A = new id in TEMPOB
07BF9	Lev1#	-> A.A = bint on level 1 masked to a library number (#7FF), after SAVPTR
07DF3	LamInFrame	B.A = frame number from 1, R0 = name -> : search that TEMPENV frame for the name; not found goes on through LrclR0A, carry set when the frame is exhausted
07ED8	ROMPTR>ADDR	A.A = a ROMPTR object -> carry clear and A.A = the object's address, or carry set if there is no such library or command (the body of ROMPTR@)
07F6E	LibAccessEnd1	-> : LibAccessEnd for a pointer read through the handler; D1 comes back from #800FA, carry set
0839B	Rrp>FirstEnt	A.A = directory -> D0 at the link of the first entry in the directory that holds it; carry set if it is HOME
08400	SKIPVARNAME	D0 at a directory entry's name (length, characters, length) -> D0 at its object
0841E	Name>Start	D0 at an object in a directory -> D0 at its name's length byte, before the name
0843A		Search ROMPTAB (RAM #809A3) by library number; used by ROMPART>ADDR
08479	LibHash@	A.X = library -> : LibTab@ with ST11 clear: the hash table
0847C	LibTab@	A.X = library, ST11 clear for the hash table, set for the link table -> D0 at it; carry set if there is none. Looks in HOME's attachment table first, then ROMPTAB
084B2	ROMPLibTab@	As LibTab@ for libraries found through ROMPTAB only
084F5	LibConfig@	A.X = library -> the library's config object, found through ROMPTAB; carry set if none
08503	Rrp>Last	A.A = directory -> D0 at its offset to the last name, past the attachment table; carry set. HOME's table has 13-nibble entries, others have only the count
0852C	DirLinksAdj	C.A = change of size, D0 = position, ST5 set for growth -> : correct every directory size link from there up to HOME
089D8	DirDelOb	ob flag -> : remove the stored object ob from its directory. FALSE discards it; other flags keep it, moved into TEMPOB. Machine code in CREATE's tail finishes
08BF3	OpenTempGapR3	R3 = nibbles: as OpenTempGap
08BF6	OpenTempGap	C.A = nibbles -> R2 = old TEMPTOP: raise the return stack, RSKTOP and TEMPTOP by that much
08C68	StoId	ob id -> : store: replace the object if the variable exists (a directory being replaced is checked first and moved out of the way), otherwise CREATE it
0966F	HomeNames@	-> A.A = HOME's offset to last name, past its attachment table
098DB	ReconfigBus	-> ST3 set if the config CRC is wrong: otherwise UNCNFG everything and CONFIG the modules again by their C=ID values, then rebuild the port status at #8052F (ST0 set if a card changed)
099C0	ConfigIfID	A.X = ID, C.A = address or size -> carry set if the next module has another ID; else CONFIG it
09A4C		Set RAMEND from the card state: a merged card in port 1 extends RAM
09B26	ConfigCard	D0 at a port's status nibble -> D0 past it: CONFIG that card by its size, at #C0000 or the bank-switch address
09B98	CardProbe	C.P = card status bits, D1 at the port's status byte -> : set bits for present, writable and size, testing RAM size through RamSizeTest
09BED	ClrPortStat	-> : zero the port status at #8052F and store its new CRC
09C05	CardBanks	Count the 128K banks of the card in port 2 by reading at each bank until the first repeats
09CE1	BankSelect	C.S and A.S = bank bits -> : select a bank through the bank switcher at #7F040+2n; 0 selects the ROM view (bit 7 of LINECOUNT)
09CFA		C.B = bank number, 0 for none
09D49	BankNone	-> : BankSelect with bank 0

8703F	PolyRootsC	[coefficients] [roots] hxs -> : PROOT core for complex coefficients. The first line sizes the workspace (54 nibbles per coefficient plus #272); R4 points at it. Header: +0 degree left, +5 degree, +10 flags, +12 result pointer. Coefficients are unpacked to extended reals
87112		Unpack each coefficient, noting the largest exponent in R2
87157		Remove roots at infinity and at 0, then scale all coefficients if the exponents are too far apart
871DF		Degree 1 and 2 are solved directly; anything else starts iterating

877F7		No root found: error #C001, library 0C0 message 1, "Unable to find root"

879A7	PRRoot	AB R0R1 -> : packs the complex root into the result array at the pointer in R4+12 and lowers the degree at R4+0. Carry set while roots remain; at degree 0 it ends the code object through GETPTRLOOP
87A04	PRStripTop	C.S = pass: removes zero leading coefficients, moving the array down, and stores a root at infinity for each (MAXR on the first pass)
87A6E	PRStripEnd	C.S = pass: removes zero constant terms, storing a root at 0 for each (a very small number on the second pass)
87AAD	PRExpLimit	A.A = exponent -> carry set if it is above 5000. Below -5000 clears AB, to avoid underflow

C78F2	DEPlotRKF	{list} ... -> : draws a Diff Eq plot, stepping with RKF. Binds the list's names, runs DEPlotRKF1 under an error trap and on the way out restores the stack depth and clears ATTN. The plotter calls it when no derivatives are given.
C795D	DEPlotRKF1	Plot loop for RKF: after DEPlotInit, takes a step at a time to the end of the range, drawing a line from the last point to the new one. Runs backwards when the final T is below the current T.
C7B1A	DEPlotInit	Common start of a Diff Eq plot. Errors if the step limit is not positive, reads the plot range and PICT scale, binds 12 locals, checks the initial solution and works out the first step size from the tolerance. Returns TRUE when the initial T already equals the final T, so there is nothing to draw.
C7DC2	DEPlotRRK	{list} ... -> : as DEPlotRKF, for a list with the derivatives FW and FT, stepping with the method chosen by RRKorRKF.
C7E2D	DEPlotRRK1	Plot loop for the stiff case: as DEPlotRKF1, carrying the last method used from step to step.
C802C		RKFERR: {list} xh -> {list} xh ydelta xerror. One RKF step of size h, leaving T and the solution unchanged.
C80D9	RKFTrap	Error handler of RKF, RKFERR and RKFSTEP: clears ATTN, restores the stack depth saved in local 5 and passes the error on.
C80F7		Binds the step's locals, recalls T and the solution W, and runs one trial step through RKFStepSel.
C8177	RKFStep%	Fehlberg 4(5) trial step for a real W, with h in local 6, t in local 8 and w in local 7. The six stages run at t, t+h/4, t+3h/8, t+12h/13, t+h and t+h/2, storing T and W for each evaluation of the expression F. Leaves the increment and the error estimate as extended reals. A step too small to change t gives an increment from the slope and an error of zero.
C87FA	RKFStep[]	The same Fehlberg step as RKFStep% for a vector W, with the arithmetic done on extended real arrays.
C8F08		RKFSTEP: {list} xtol xh -> {list} xtol xhnext. One adaptive step, under an error trap.
C8F83		Errors if the step is not positive, then runs DEStepCtl once with the trial step chosen by RKFStepSel.
C8FEF	RKFStepSel	w -> stepcode: picks RKFStep% for a real W, RKFStep[] for a vector, and errors (501, Invalid Dimension) on a matrix.
C9046	DEStepCtl	stepcode -> hnext: adaptive step control. Runs the trial step and compares the error with the tolerance. While the error is too large it shrinks h by .9*(tol/err)^.2, no further than a tenth on the first failure, and by .2 after that. Then stores the new T and W and proposes the next step from the same formula, growing it by at most 5 times.
C9248	DoRKF	RKF: {list} xtol xTfinal -> {list} xtol, where xtol may be {xtol xh}. Solves up to xTfinal under an error trap.
C92AF	DoRKF1	Stepping loop of RKF: repeats DEStepCtl until T reaches the final T. Does nothing when T is already there.
C939A	DEStepArgs	Reads the tolerance, optional first step and final T. Errors if the tolerance is not positive. Returns TRUE when T already equals the final T. With no step given, the first step comes from (tol/|F|)^.2; it takes the sign of the direction of travel.
C953A	RKFBindList	{T W F ...} x -> : checks that the list has at least three elements, of which the first two are names, and binds them as the locals T, W and F above two unnamed ones.

C97B5	RRKStep%	Rosenbrock trial step for a real W, using the derivatives FW and FT. Falls back on RKFStep% when h is too small to change t. Divides by 1-FW*h/2, taken as 1E-14 when that is zero.

CA25D		RRKSTEP: {list} xtol xh xlast -> {list} xtol xhnext xcurrent. One adaptive step by the better method. xlast and xcurrent are 1 for RKF and 2 for Rosenbrock.

CA411	RRKorRKF	Chooses the method for one step, given the last method used. Tries Rosenbrock steps while their error meets the tolerance (RRKAccept), and otherwise takes an RKF step. Leaves the method used as a real, 1 for RKF.
CA828	RRKAccept	A Rosenbrock step met the tolerance: works out the next step from .9*(tol/err)^.25, stores the new T and W and returns the step with 2, the code for Rosenbrock.

D427D		Receive: takes all free memory but 2000 nibbles for a string to hold the data, with interrupts off. R2 holds the block number in its low byte and the retries left in nibble 2.
